Up-to-boundary pointwise gradient estimates for very singular quasilinear elliptic equations with mixed data Thanks: ∗ Corresponding author
Tan Duc Do, Le Xuan Truong, Nguyen Ngoc Trong ∗

Abstract
. This paper establishes pointwise estimates up to boundary for the gradient of weak solutions to a class of very singular quasilinear elliptic equations with mixed data { − div ⁡ ( 𝐀 ⁡ ( x , D ​ u ) ) = g − div ⁡ f in Ω u = 0 on ​ ∂ Ω , \begin{cases}-\operatorname{div}\left(\mathbf{A}(x,Du)\right)=g-\operatorname{div}f\quad&\mathrm{in}\quad\Omega\\ u=0\quad&\text{on}\ \partial\Omega,\end{cases} where Ω ⊂ ℝ n \Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n} is sufficiently flat in the sense of Reifenberg.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
primary: 35J60, 35J61, 35J62; secondary: 35J75, 42B37

1.
Introduction

Regularity for solutions to
p
-Laplacian equations is classical, yet in itself is an interesting topic.
Generally speaking, the theory is up to date well-known with various results fully established.
This naturally sparks extensions to more general settings of
p
-Laplacian type equations which have now become an active area of research.
For a partial overview on its mainstream development, one may take into account pioneering works such as
[
9
]
,
[
16
]
,
[
22
]
,
[
27
]
,
[
28
]
,
[
29
]
,
[
30
]
,
[
31
]
,
[
48
]
,
[
49
]
,
[
50
]
,
[
36
]
,
[
38
]
and
[
41
]
(see also their references therein).
Specifically, these papers discussed in details the interior
C^{1,\alpha}
regularity for weak solutions to
p
-Laplacian equations with different types of data and to homogeneous quasilinear elliptic equations of the form
-\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{A}(x,u,Du))=0
.

In this paper, we pay particular attention to quasilinear elliptic equations formulated in a general form

\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{A}(x,Du))=G,

where
G
may take on either divergence form, non-divergence form or even a measure value.
Some regularity results for solutions to such equations are readily available.
Namely, interior and global regularities are known for
\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{A}(x,Du))=\operatorname{div}(|F|^{p-2}F)
with zero
Dirichlet boundary data.
These are due to the seminal works of Byun et al. in
[
5
]
,
[
6
]
,
[
7
]
as well as those of Mengesha et al and Nguyen in
[
33
]
,
[
34
]
,
[
37
]
in the setting of (weighted or unweighted) Lebesgue and Sobolev spaces.
When the divergence form
\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{A}(x,Du))=\operatorname{div}(F)
is considered, interior
W^{1,q}
estimates were obtained by Nguyen et al. in
[
43
]
.
Note that these works land themselves in the expected setting of either the ellipticity condition of
\mathbf{A}
or a requirement of small
BMO
oscillation in
x
.
Besides, the underlying geometric framework involves, in its most general sense, Reifenberg flat domains
\Omega
.
Analogous results still remain when those settings are weaken or vary in certain manners.
For example, one can take into account
[
45
]
for a weaker hypothesis on
\Omega
in which a
p
-capacity uniformly thickness condition is used and for solutions belonging to Lorentz spaces.
Recently
[
18
]
and
[
42
]
studied the global gradient estimates in weighted Morrey spaces, which generalize
[
43
]
.
Other results under different assumptions on
\Omega
, the nonlinearity
\mathbf{A}
and the boundary data can be found in
[
2
]
,
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
8
]
,
[
19
]
,
[
25
]
,
[
41
]
.
When a measure-valued
G
is considered, interior pointwise gradient estimates via Riesz and Wolff potentials were obtained in
[
14
]
and
[
15
]
for growth exponents in the ranges
(2-\frac{1}{n},\infty)
and
(2,\infty)
respectively.
Beside quasilinear equations, there is also a growing interest in system settings which are much more involved.
We refer the readers to
[
10
]
,
[
11
]
,
[
12
]
,
[
18
]
,
[
26
]
,
[
13
]
,
[
38
]
,
[
39
]
,
[
40
]
,
[
41
]
,
[
36
]
and
[
37
]
and their references therein for further exploration in this direction.

The aforegoing discussion leads us to consider a gradient estimate up to boundary for the solutions to a very singular quasilinear elliptic equation with mixed data.
More precisely, let
n\in\{2,3,4,\ldots\}
and
\Omega
be a bounded open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Let
p\in(1,n)
.
Consider

(1)

\begin{cases}-\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{A}(x,Du))=g-\operatorname{div}f\quad&\mathrm{in}\quad\Omega,\\
u=0\quad&\text{on}\ \partial\Omega,\end{cases}

in which

g\in L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})\quad\mbox{and}\quad f\in L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{n}).

The nonlinearity

\mathbf{A}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}

is a Caratheodory function in the sense that
\mathbf{A}(x,z)
is measurable in
x
for every
z
and is continuous in
z
for a.e.
x
.
Moreover,
\mathbf{A}(x,z)
is differentiable in
z
away from the origin for a.e.
x
.
The following
growth, ellipticity
and
continuity assumptions
are also imposed on
\mathbf{A}
.
Suppose there exists a
\Lambda\geq 1
such that

(2)

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}(x,z)|\leq\Lambda\,|z|^{p-1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad|D_{2}\mathbf{A}(x,z)|\leq\Lambda\,|z|^{p-2},

(3)

\displaystyle\langle D_{2}\mathbf{A}(x,z)\,\eta,\eta\rangle\geq\Lambda^{-1}\,|z|^{p-2}\,|\eta|^{2},

\displaystyle|D_{2}\mathbf{A}(x,z)-D_{2}\mathbf{A}(x,\eta)|\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Lambda\,|z|^{p-2}\,|\eta|^{p-2}\,(|z|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})^{(2-p-\alpha)/2}\,|z-\eta|^{\alpha}&\mbox{ if }p<2,\\
\Lambda\,(|z|^{2}+|\eta|^{2})^{(p-2-\alpha)/2}\,|z-\eta|^{\alpha}&\mbox{ if }p\geq 2\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\mbox{for some }\alpha\in(0,|2-p|)\mbox{ if }p\neq 2\mbox{ and }\alpha\in(0,1]\mbox{ if }p=2,

(7)

\displaystyle|\mathbf{A}(x,z)-\mathbf{A}(x_{0},z)|\leq\Lambda\,\omega(|x-x_{0}|)\,|z|^{p-1}

for all
x,x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and for all
(z,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
, where
D_{2}\mathbf{A}(x,z)
denotes the Jacobian matrix of
\mathbf{A}
with respect to the second variable
z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\}.

As usual the function
\omega:[0,\infty)\longrightarrow[0,1]
in (
7
) is required to be non-decreasing and satisfies

\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\omega(r)=\omega(0)=0

as well as the Dini’s condition

(8)

\int_{0}^{1}\omega(r)^{\tau_{0}}\frac{dr}{r}:=Z<\infty,

where
\tau_{0}:=\frac{2}{p}\wedge 1
.

The above conditions on
n
,
p
,
\Omega
,
\mathbf{A}
,
f
and
g
(as well as the parameters therein) serve as the background of our paper and will be imposed without being mentioned in all statements throughout.
Further assumptions if required will be explicitly articulated in the corresponding statements.

Certain nice consequences can be drawn from the above conditions.
The first inequality in (
2
) and the Caratheodory property together yield

\mathbf{A}(x,0)=0\quad\mbox{for a.e. }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Meanwhile (
3
) implies the strict monotonicity condition

(9)

\left\langle\mathbf{A}(x,z)-\mathbf{A}(x,\eta),z-\eta\right\rangle\geq C(n,p,\Lambda)\,\Phi(z,\eta)

for all
(z,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
and for a.e.
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined by

\Phi(z,\eta):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(|z|^{2}+|\eta|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}|z-\eta|^{2}&\mbox{if }p\leq 2,\\
|z-\eta|^{p}&\mbox{if }p>2\end{array}\right.

due to
[
48
, Lemma 1]
.

A typical model for (
1
) is obviously given by the
p
-Laplace equation with mixed data

-\Delta_{p}\,u:=-{\rm div}(|Du|^{p-2}Du)=g-\operatorname{div}f\quad\text{in~}\Omega.

Note that this special case of ours is very much in line with
[
41
]
in which the
p
-Laplace equation with mixed data of the form
\operatorname{div}(|F|^{p-2}F)+f
and homogeneous Dirichlet boundary condition is investigated. Moreover, the parabolic version of (
1
) is also considered in
[
36
]
and
[
37
]
.

Our aim here is to derive pointwise estimates for the gradient of solutions to (
1
) up to the boundary of
\Omega
.
The main contribution involves the reconstructions of a Holder continuity estimate for the solutions to (
1
), a comparison estimate, an iteration lemma and a Gehring’s lemma peculiar to the setting in this paper.

To state our main result, we first need some definitions.

Definition 1.1
.

A function
u\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
is a solution to (
1
) if

\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{A}(x,Du)\cdot D\varphi\,dx=\int_{\Omega}f\cdot D\varphi\,dx+\int_{\Omega}g\,\varphi\,dx

for all
\varphi\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega).

In what follows, for each measurable function
h:\Omega\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}
denote

(h)_{B}=\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B}h(x)\,dx=\frac{1}{|B|}\int_{B}h(x)\,dx.

The oscillation of
h
on a set
A\subset\Omega
is defined by

\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{A}h:=\mathop{\sup}\limits_{x,y\in A}\big(h(x)-h(y)\big)=\sup_{x\in A}h(x)-\inf_{x\in A}h(x).

Also set

{\bf F}^{R}_{q}(f,g)(x):=\int_{0}^{R}\left[\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}|f-(f)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}|^{q^{\prime}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\rho^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\,\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}|g|^{\frac{nq}{nq-n+q}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{nq-n+q}{(nq-n)q}}\right]\,\frac{d\rho}{\rho}

for each
q\in(1,n)
and
R>0
, where
q^{\prime}
is the conjugate index of
q
.

Our first main result is as follows.

Theorem 1.2
.

Suppose that
u\in C^{1}(\Omega)
solves (
1
).
Then

|Du(x)|\leq C\left[{\bf F}^{R}_{p}(f,g)(x)+\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{R}(x)}|Du(y)|^{p}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right]

for all ball
B_{R}(x)\subset\Omega
, where
C=C(n,p,\Lambda,Z)
.

It is worth mentioning that, in comparison to
[
14
]
and
[
15
]
, Theorem 1.2 provides an interior gradient estimate for all
p\in(1,n)
.
The proof of Theorem
1.2
makes use of the following
C^{1,\kappa}
-regularity estimate for the associated homogeneous equation.
This result is also of independent interest.

Theorem 1.3
.

Set
\mathbf{A}_{0}=\mathbf{A}(0,\cdot)
.
Let
v\in W^{1,p}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)
be a weak solution of

(10)

{\rm div}\,\mathbf{A}_{0}(Dv)=0\quad\mbox{in }\Omega.

Then there exist constants
C\geq 1
,
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
\sigma_{0}\in(0,1/2]
, all of which depend on
n
and
p
only such that

(11)

\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{B_{\delta r}}Dv\leq C\,\delta^{\kappa}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx

for all
\delta\in(0,\sigma_{0}]
and for all ball
B_{r}\subset\Omega
.

Next we state the pointwise estimate up to the boundary of
\Omega
for a solution
u
of (
1
).
This is possible provided that
\partial\Omega
is sufficiently flat in the sense of Reifenberg.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
and
R_{0}>0
.
Then
\Omega
is called a
(\delta,R_{0})
-Reifenberg flat domain if for all
x\in\partial\Omega
and
r\in(0,R_{0}]
there exists a system of coordinates
\{z_{1},z_{2},...,z_{n}\}
, depending on
r
and
x
, in which one has
x=0
and

B_{r}(0)\cap\{z_{n}>\delta r\}\subset B_{r}(0)\cap\Omega\subset B_{r}(0)\cap\{z_{n}>-\delta r\}.

This class of domains first appeared in the work of Reifenberg
[
47
]
in the context of Plateau problems.
The class is broad enough to include
C^{1}
domains, Lipschitz domains with sufficiently small Lipschitz constants and certain fractal domains.
Its significance has been solidified in the theory of minimal surfaces and free boundary problems.
For many interesting properties of Reifenberg flat domains, we refer the readers to
[
23
]
and
[
24
]
.

Our pointwise estimate up to the boundary of
\Omega
is as follows.

Theorem 1.5
.

Suppose that
u
is a solution of (
1
).
Then there exists a
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
such that

(12)

\displaystyle|Du(x)|\leq Cd(x)^{-\zeta/p}\,{\bf F}^{2\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)}_{p}(f,g)(x)

for a.e.
x\in\Omega
and for all
\zeta\in(0,n)
, where
d(x):=\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)
.

We remark that it is in general not possible to take
\zeta=0
in (
12
) due to a potential irregularity of
\Omega
. Beside, (
12
) is proved in
[
39
]
when
f\equiv 0
and
g
is a finite signed measure
in
\Omega
.

The outline of the paper is as follows.
Proposition
1.3
is proved in Section
2
.
Section
3
discusses a comparison estimate and an iteration lemma which are then used in the proof of the interior pointwise gradient estimate - Theorem
1.2
.
The global pointwise gradient estimate - Theorem
1.5
is obtained in Section
4
.

Notation.
Throughout the paper the following set of notation is used without mentioning.
Set
\mathbb{N}=\{0,1,2,3,\ldots\}
and
\mathbb{N}^{*}=\{1,2,3,\ldots\}
.
For all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,
a\wedge b=\min\{a,b\}
and
a\vee b=\max\{a,b\}
.
For all ball
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
we write
w(B):=\int_{B}w
.
The constants
C
and
c
are always assumed to be positive and independent of the main parameters whose values change from line to line.
Given a ball
B=B_{r}(x)
, we let
tB=B_{tr}(x)
for all
t>0
.
The conjugate index of
p\in[1,\infty)
is denoted by
p^{\prime}
.

2.
A
C^{1,\kappa}
-regularity estimate

We start by proving Theorem
1.3
.
This paves way for the proof of Theorem
1.2
later, but is also of independent interest.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We follow the arguments used in
[
26
, Proof of Theorem 3.2]
closely.

By
[
9
, Proposition 3.3]
, for all
\delta\in(0,1)
there exists a constant
C=C(n,p,\delta)>0
such that

\sup_{B_{\delta r}}|Dv|\leq C\,\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|^{p}\,dx\right)^{1/p},

which in turn yields

(13)

\sup_{B_{\delta r}}|Dv|\leq C\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|\,dx

due to
[
44
, Lemmas 2.1 and 2.4]
.

With (
13
) in mind, one can then apply standard arguments to obtain a
C^{1,\kappa}
a priori local estimate (cf.
[
32
, Theorem 2, p.15]
).
Specifically, there exist constants
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,p)\geq 1
and
\beta_{0}=\beta_{0}(n,p)\in(0,1)
such that

(14)

\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{B_{\delta r}}Dv\leq C_{0}\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|\,dx

for all
\delta\in(0,2/3)
.

Now we consider two cases.

Case 1
: Suppose

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx\geq\theta_{0}\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|,

where

(15)

\theta_{0}:=\frac{\sigma_{0}^{n}}{40}\in(0,1)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sigma_{0}:=\left(\frac{1}{160C_{0}}\right)^{1/\beta_{0}}\in\left(0,\frac{1}{160}\right).

Then (
14
) gives

\displaystyle\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{B_{\delta r}}Dv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx+C_{0}\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\left(1+\frac{1}{\theta_{0}}\right)\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx

for all
\delta\in(0,2/3)
.
Therefore (
11
) holds with
\sigma_{0}=1/2
.

Case 2
: Suppose

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx<\theta_{0}\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|,

where
\theta_{0}
is as in (
15
).
To avoid triviality, assume further that
|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|>0
.
Then

(16)

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|\,dx\leq 2\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|,

by the triangle inequality.
Combining this estimate with (
14
) and the definition of
\sigma_{0}
leads to

(17)

\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{B_{2\sigma_{0}r}}Dv\leq 4C_{0}\sigma_{0}^{\beta_{0}}\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\leq|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|/20.

At the same time, one also has

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2\sigma_{0}r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx\leq(2\sigma_{0})^{-n}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx\leq(2\sigma_{0})^{-n}\,\theta_{0}\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|.

This ensures the existence of an
\tilde{x}\in B_{2\sigma_{0}r}
such that

(18)

|Dv(\tilde{x})-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\leq|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|/20.

Now (
17
) and (
18
) together imply

(19)

|Dv(x)-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\leq|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|/10

for all
x\in B_{2\sigma_{0}r}
.

On the other hand, we infer from (
13
) and (
16
) that there exists a
c=c(n,p)>0
such that

(20)

|Dv(x)|\leq c\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|\leq 2c\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|

for all
x\in B_{2r/3}
.

Combining (
19
) and (
20
) yields

(21)

|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|/c\leq|Dv(x)|\leq c\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|

for some
c=c(n,p)
and for all
x\in B_{2\sigma_{0}r}
.

Next we take (
14
) and (
16
) into account to derive

(22)

|Dv(x)-Dv(y)|\leq c(n,p)\,|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,|x-y|^{\beta_{0}}

for all
x,y\in B_{r/2}
.
By partially differentiating (
10
) and performing a scaling on the coefficients, the partial derivatives
w_{i}:=\partial_{x_{i}}v
satisfy

-{\rm div}\left(\frac{\mathbf{A}_{0}^{\prime}(Dv)}{|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|^{p-2}}\,Dw_{i}\right)=0,

which is a uniformly elliptic equation due to (
2
), (
3
) and (
21
).
Furthermore we deduce from (
1
) and (
22
) that there exists a
c=c(n,p)>0
satisfying

\left|\frac{\mathbf{A}_{0}^{\prime}(Dv)(x)}{|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|^{p-2}}-\frac{\mathbf{A}_{0}^{\prime}(Dv)(y)}{|(Dv)_{B_{r}}|^{p-2}}\right|\leq c\,|x-y|^{\alpha\,\beta_{0}}

for all
x,y\in B_{2\sigma_{0}r}
, where
\alpha
is given by (
1
).

Consequently a standard result provides that
w_{i}
is locally
\gamma
-Holder continuous in
B_{\sigma_{0}r}
for all exponent
\gamma<1
.
Particularly, there exists a
c=c(n,p)>0
such that

\mathop{\operatorname{osc}}\limits_{B_{\delta r}}w_{i}\leq c\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2\sigma_{0}r}}|w_{i}-(w_{i})_{B_{\sigma_{0}r}}|\,dx\leq 2c\sigma_{0}^{-n}\,\delta^{\beta_{0}}\,\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|w_{i}-(Dv)_{B_{r}}|\,dx

for all
\delta\in(0,\sigma_{0})
, where the last step is justified by
[
26
, (2.1)]
.
Whence the claim follows for all
\delta\in(0,\sigma_{0}]
.

This completes our proof.

As a consequence of Theorem
1.3
, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 2.1
.

Set
\mathbf{A}_{0}=\mathbf{A}(0,\cdot)
.
Let
v\in W^{1,p}_{\rm loc}(\Omega)
be a weak solution of

{\rm div}\,\mathbf{A}_{0}(Dv)=0\quad\mbox{in }\Omega.

Then there exist constants
C\geq 1
,
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
\sigma_{0}\in(0,1/2]
, all of which depend on
n
and
p
only, such that

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{\rho}(x_{0})}|^{q}\,dx\right)^{1/q}\leq C\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\kappa}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}|Dv-(Dv)_{B_{R}(x_{0})}|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}

for all
q\in[1,\infty)
,
B_{R}(x_{0})\subset\Omega
and
0<\rho<\sigma_{0}R
.

3.
Interior pointwise gradient estimates

The main goal of this section is to prove Theorem
1.2
.
To this end, we first derive several preliminary results.

Let
u\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
be a solution of (
1
).
Suppose that
B_{2r}=B_{2r}(x_{0})\Subset\Omega
.

In this whole section, let
w\in u+W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{2r})
be the unique solution to the problem

(23)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbf{A}(x,Dw)}\right)&=&0\quad{\rm in}\quad B_{2r},\\
w&=&u\quad{\rm on}\quad\partial B_{2r}\end{array}\right.

and let
v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(B_{r}(x_{0}))
be the unique solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbf{A}(x_{0},Dv)}\right)&=&0\quad{\rm in}\quad B_{r},\\
v&=&w\quad{\rm on}\quad\partial B_{r}.\end{array}\right.

Several convenient properties of
w
and
v
can immediately listed here.

As a result of
[
44
, Lemmas 2.1 and 2.4]
(also cf.
[
39
, (3.3)]
), there exists a
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
such that

(24)

\displaystyle\|Dv\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{r/2})}\leq C\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|^{t}dx\right)^{1/t}

for all
t>0
.

We also have an estimate for the difference
Dv-Dw
as follows.
There exists a
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
such that

(25)

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip .

Proof.

.
Then

(26)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{B_{2r}}\big[A(x,Du)-A(x,Dw)\big]\cdot D\varphi\,dx=\int_{B_{2r}}(f-m)\cdot D\varphi\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}g\,\varphi\,dx

for all

Choosing

as a test function in (

\displaystyle\displaystyle\int_{B_{2r}}\big[A(x,Du)-A(x,Dw)\big]\cdot D(u-w)dx=\int_{B_{2r}}(f-m)\cdot D(u-w)dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}g\,(u-w)\,dx.

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,\Lambda)\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}dx\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|Du-Dw|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\quad{}+C(n,p,\Lambda)\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}dx\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np}}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|u-w|^{\frac{np}{n-p}}dx\right)^{\frac{n-p}{np}}.

Next we apply Sobolev’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|u-w|^{\frac{np}{n-p}}dx\right)^{\frac{n-p}{np}}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p)\,r\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|Du-Dw|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}.

Consequently,

(27)

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}\Phi(Dw,Du)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,\Lambda)\left[\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}dx\right)^{1/p^{\prime}}+r\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}dx\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|Du-Dw|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Now we consider two cases.

Case 1
: Suppose
p\in(1,2)
.
Then rewrite

(28)

\displaystyle|Du-Dw|^{p}

\displaystyle=\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}\left(|Du|+|Du-Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}|Du|^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}\left(|Du-Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}.

Using Young’s inequality in the form

ab^{\frac{2-p}{2}}\leq\frac{p\epsilon^{\frac{p-2}{p}}a^{\frac{2}{p}}}{2}+\frac{(2-p)\epsilon b}{2}

with an appropriate
\epsilon>0
, we arrive at

\displaystyle|Du-Dw|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}|Du|^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\,(|Dw|+|Du|)^{p-2}|D(u-w)|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|Du-Dw|^{p},

whence it follows from (
28
) that

\displaystyle|Du-Dw|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\,(|Dw|+|Du|)^{p-2}|D(u-w)|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(p)\,\left(|Du|+|Dw|\right)^{\frac{p(p-2)}{2}}|Du-Dw|^{p}|Du|^{\frac{p(2-p)}{2}}.

By integrating both sides of the above estimate on
B_{2r}
and the apply Holder’s inequality with exponents
\frac{2}{p}
and
\frac{2}{2-p}>1
, we obtain

(29)

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|D(u-w)|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}(|Dw|+|Du|)^{p-2}|D(u-w)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle+C(p)\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}(|Dw|+|Du|)^{p-2}|D(u-w)|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|Du|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{2-p}{2}}.

Combining (
27
) with (
29
) yields

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|D(u-w)|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\leq C(n,p,\Lambda)\left[\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip \displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}}|Du-Dw|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\quad+C(n,p,\Lambda)\left[\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip as required, where we used Young’s inequality in the second step.

This completes the proof.

3.2.
Iteration lemma

Another key ingredient in the proof of Theorem
1.2
is the iteration lemma given by Proposition
3.2
below.

In what follows, define

\mathbf{I}(\rho)=\mathbf{I}(x_{0},\rho):=\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}}|Du-\left(Du\right)_{B_{\rho}}|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}

for a ball
B_{\rho}=B_{\rho}(x_{0})\subset\Omega
.

.

Remark 3.3
.

If

then we may choose

in Proposition

.

Proof.

By virtue of Lemma
2.1
, there exist constants

,
\kappa\in(0,1)
and
\sigma_{0}\in(0,1/2]
, all of which depend on

and

only, such that

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\delta r}}|Du-\left(Du\right)_{B_{\delta r}}|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip ) follows after an application of Young’s inequality as we did in the last part of Case 2.

4.
Global pointwise gradient estimates

This section considers a boundary counterpart of Theorem
1.2
.
The main task is to prove Theorem
1.5
.

Before proceeding further with details, let us simplify the procedure of proving Theorem
1.5
as follows.
By using a standard approximating procedure we may assume that
u\in C^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
is a solution of (
1
).
Let
x_{0}\in\Omega
.
Then Theorem
1.2
states that there exists a
C=C(n,p,\Lambda,Z)>0
satisfying

(36)

\displaystyle|Du(x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C\,{\bf F}^{2\rm diam(\Omega)}_{p}(f,g)(x)+C\,\Big(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{d(x_{0})}(x_{0})}|Du(y)|^{p}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Also we infer from Lemma

that

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip with
