¿ 0.

These two estimates together allow us to assume that
d(x_{0})\leq r_{1}/2
for some sufficiently small
r_{1}\in(0,1)
.
Recall that
\Omega
is a
(\delta,R_{0})
-Reifenberg flat domain for some
R_{0}>0
.
Therefore we may assume further that

d(x_{0})\leq\frac{r_{1}}{2}\leq\frac{R_{0}}{100}\leq\frac{{\rm diam}(\Omega)}{1000}.

This condition is implicitly understood for the rest of the paper.

4.1.
Estimates near the boundary

The following notation is fixed in this whole section.
Let
x_{1}\in\partial\Omega
be such that
|x_{1}-x_{0}|=d(x_{0})
.
Let
r\in(0,r_{1}]
.

Let
w\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega_{2r}(x_{1}))+u
be the unique solution
to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbf{A}(x,Dw)}\right)&=&0\quad{\rm in}~~\Omega_{2r}(x_{1}),\\
w&=&u\quad{\rm on}~~\partial\Omega_{2r}(x_{1}),\end{array}\right.

where we write
\Omega_{r}(x_{1})=\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{1})
.

Next let
v\in w+W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega_{r}(x_{1}))
be the unique solution to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}-\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbf{A}(x_{1},Dv)}\right)&=&0\quad{\rm in}~~\Omega_{r}(x_{1}),\\
v&=&w\quad{\rm on}~~\partial\Omega_{r}(x_{1}).\end{array}\right.

In what follows, we always tacitly extend
u
by zero to
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Omega
, whence in turn extend
w
by
u
to

\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Omega_{2r}(x_{1})
and
v
by
w
to

\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Omega_{r}(x_{1})
.

In this subsection we collects several technical lemmas involving
w
and
v
.

A slight modification in the proof of (
25
) (to adapt the boundary case) verifies that

(38)

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}(x_{1})}|Dv-Dw|^{p}dx\leq C(n,p,\Lambda)\,\omega(r)^{\tau_{0}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}(x_{1})}|Dw|^{p}dx.

A boundary counterpart of (
24
) is more involved and so we include a proof.
To this end, the following well-known property is essential, cf.
[
51
, Theorem 3]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
0<\epsilon<1
and
B_{R}
be a ball of radius
R
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Let
E\subset F\subset B_{R}
be two measurable sets with the following properties:

(1)

|E|<\epsilon|B_{R}|
and

(2)

for all
x\in B_{R}
and
\rho\in(0,R]
, if
|E\cap B_{\rho}(x)|\geq\epsilon|B_{\rho}(x)|
then
B_{\rho}(x)\cap B_{R}\subset F
.

Then there exists a constant
C=C(n)
such that
|E|\leq C\epsilon|F|
.

The boundary counterpart of (
24
) is as follows.
Observe that the estimate appears in an integral form as opposed to the pointwise estimate in (
24
).
This compensates the possible irregularity which may occur to the boundary of
\Omega
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
q>p
. There exists a
\delta=\delta(q)>0
such that if
\Omega
is a
(\delta,R_{0})
-Reifenberg flat domain then

(39)

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r/800}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{q}dx\right)^{1/q}\leq C\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
. In particular,

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\epsilon r}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx\leq C\epsilon^{-\frac{pn}{q}}\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{r}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx

for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/800)
.

Proof.

The argument presented here is inspired by
[
39
, Proof of Lemma 4.2]
, also see
[
36
]
,
[
37
]
,
[
38
]
,
[
40
]
.

Assume that
\Omega
is a
(\delta,R_{0})
-Reifenberg flat domain.
We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1
: Let
{\bf M}
be the standard Hardy-Littlewood maximal function and write
\mathbf{1}_{E}
to denote the characteristic function of a set
E
. Set
\rho=r/800
and for
\lambda>0
let

E_{\lambda}=\left\{z\in\Omega:\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)(z)\right]^{1/p}>\lambda\right\}\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1}).

Our task here is to show that for all
\epsilon>0
there exist constants

\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(n,p,\Lambda,\epsilon)\in(0,1),\quad\delta_{2}=\delta_{2}(n,p,\Lambda,\epsilon)\in(0,1)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Lambda_{0}=\Lambda_{0}(n,p,\Lambda)>1

such that if
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
then

(40)

|E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}|\leq C(n)\epsilon|E_{\lambda}|

for all

\lambda\geq T_{0}:=\delta_{2}^{-1}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{800\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

To this end let
\epsilon>0
.
Note that
{\bf M}:L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\longrightarrow L^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is bounded.
Therefore

(41)

\displaystyle\left|E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}\right|\leq\frac{C(n)}{(\Lambda_{0}\lambda)^{p}}\int_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx\leq C(n)\left(\frac{\delta_{2}}{\Lambda_{0}}\right)^{p}|B_{800\rho}(x_{1})|\leq\epsilon\left|B_{\rho}(x_{1})\right|

for all
\lambda\geq T_{0}
, provided that
\delta_{2}\leq\left(\frac{800^{-n}\epsilon}{C(n)}\right)^{1/p}\Lambda_{0}
.

Next we need to verify

(42)

\left|E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}\cap B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\right|\geq\epsilon\left|B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\right|\quad\Longrightarrow\quad B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})\subset E_{\lambda}

for all
x\in B_{\rho}(x_{1})
,
\rho_{1}\in(0,\rho]
and
\lambda\geq T_{0}
, provided that
\delta
and
\delta_{2}
, which depend on
n
,
p
,
\Lambda
and
\epsilon
, are sufficiently small.

Once this is done, (
40
) will follow by using (
41
), (
42
) in combination with Lemma
4.1
whose
E:=E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}
and
F:=E_{\lambda}
.

Now we prove (
42
) by contraposition.
Take
x\in B_{\rho}(x_{1})
,
\rho_{1}\in(0,\rho]
and
\lambda\geq T_{0}
.
Assume that

B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})\cap(E_{\lambda})^{c}\not=\emptyset.

That is, there exists an
x_{2}\in B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})
such that

(43)

\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)(x_{2})\right]^{1/p}\leq\lambda.

We aim to obtain

(44)

\left|E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}\cap B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\right|<\epsilon\left|B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\right|.

Clearly,

\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)(y)\right]^{1/p}\leq\max\left\{\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)}|Dv|^{p}\big)(y)\right]^{\frac{1}{p}},3^{\frac{n}{p}}\lambda\right\}

for all
y\in B_{\rho_{1}}(x)
.

It follows that

E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}\cap B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\subset\left\{z\in\Omega:\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)}|Dv|^{p}\big)(z)\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}>\Lambda_{0}\lambda\right\}\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})\cap B_{\rho_{1}}(x)

for all
\lambda\geq T_{0}
and
\Lambda_{0}\geq 3^{\frac{n}{p}}
.

We consider two cases.

Case 1
: Suppose
B_{4\rho_{1}}(x)\Subset\Omega
.

Since

\operatorname{div}\left({\mathbf{A}(x_{1},Dv)}\right)=0\quad\mbox{in }B_{4\rho_{1}}(x),

it follows from (
24
) that

\|Dv\|_{L^{\infty}\left(B_{3\rho_{1}}(x)\right)}\leq C_{1}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{4\rho_{1}}(x)}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq C_{1}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{5\rho_{1}}(x_{2})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,p,\Lambda)>0
.
This estimate together with (
43
) give

\|Dv\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3\rho_{1}}(x))}\leq C_{1}\lambda.

In particular, if
\Lambda_{0}\geq\max\left\{3^{\frac{n}{p}},4C_{1}\right\}
then

\|Dv\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3\rho_{1}}(x))}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Lambda_{0}\lambda

and so

E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}=\emptyset.

Hence (
44
) is vacuously true.

Case 2
: Suppose
\overline{B_{4\rho_{1}}(x)}\cap\Omega^{c}\not=\emptyset
.

Let
x_{3}\in\partial\Omega
be such that
|x_{3}-x|=\text{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)
. We have

B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)\subset B_{6\rho_{1}}(x_{3})\subset B_{600\rho_{1}}(x_{3})\subset B_{605\rho_{1}}(x_{2}).

Thanks to
[
35
, Theorem 2.9]
, for any
\eta>0
there exists
\delta_{1}=\delta_{1}(n,p,\Lambda,\eta)
be such that the following holds. If
\delta\leq\delta_{1}
then there exists a function
\tilde{v}\in W^{1,\infty}(B_{6\rho_{1}}(x_{3}))
such that

\|D\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{6\rho_{1}}(x_{3}))}\leq C_{2}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{600\rho_{1}}(x_{3})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p},

where
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,p,\Lambda)>0
and

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{6\rho_{1}}(x_{3})}|D(v-\tilde{v})|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\eta\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{600\rho_{1}}(x_{3})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}.

Note that if
\rho_{1}\leq\rho/100
, then

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{600\rho_{1}}(x_{3})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\leq 2^{\frac{n}{p}}\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)(x_{2})\right]^{1/p}\leq 2^{\frac{n}{p}+1}\lambda,

and if
\rho_{1}\geq\rho/100
, then since
\rho_{1}\leq\rho
,

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{600\rho_{1}}(x_{3})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\leq 10^{\frac{3n}{p}}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{800\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}dx\right)^{1/p}\leq 10^{\frac{3n}{p}}\delta_{2}\lambda.

Hence,

\|D\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}\left(B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)\right)}\leq 10^{\frac{3n}{p}}C_{2}\lambda,

and

\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)}|D(v-\tilde{v})|^{p}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq 10^{\frac{4n}{p}}\eta\lambda.

Choosing
\Lambda_{0}=\max\left\{3^{\frac{n}{p}},4C_{1},2^{\frac{1}{p}}10^{\frac{3n}{p}}C_{2}\right\}
, we derive

\displaystyle|E_{\Lambda_{0}\lambda}\cap B_{\rho_{1}}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\{\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)}|D(v-\tilde{v})|^{p}\big)\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}>2^{-\frac{1}{p}}\Lambda_{0}\lambda\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(n)}{\left(2^{-\frac{1}{p}}\Lambda_{0}\lambda\right)^{p}}\int_{B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)}|D(v-\tilde{v})|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C(n)}{\left(\Lambda_{0}\lambda\right)^{p}}\left(10^{\frac{4n}{p}}\eta\lambda\right)^{p}|B_{2\rho_{1}}(x)|

\displaystyle<\epsilon\left|B_{\rho_{1}}(x)\right|,

for

\eta=\left(\frac{\epsilon}{10^{5n}C(n)}\right)^{1/p}.

This gives (
44
).

Step 2
: We will prove (
39
).

Set
\lambda_{0}=\Lambda_{0}T_{0}
.
Thanks to (
40
),

(45)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)\right]^{q/p}dx

\displaystyle=q\int_{0}^{\infty}\lambda^{q-1}\,\left|\left\{\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)\right]^{1/p}>\lambda\right\}\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})\right|\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq q\int_{0}^{\lambda_{0}}\lambda^{q-1}\,|B_{\rho}(x_{1})|\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+C(n)\,q\epsilon\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{\infty}\lambda^{q-1}\,\left|\left\{\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)\right]^{1/p}>\lambda/\Lambda_{0}\right\}\cap B_{\rho}(x_{1})\right|\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{0}^{q}\,|B_{\rho}(x_{1})|+C(n)\,\Lambda_{0}^{q}\epsilon\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)\right]^{q/p}dx.

A limiting argument then justifies that

\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}\left[{\bf M}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B_{8\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{p}\big)\right]^{q/p}dx<\infty.

With this in mind, we choose
\epsilon=\frac{1}{2C(n)\Lambda_{0}^{q}}
in (
45
) to obtain

\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}|Dv|^{q}dx\leq\lambda_{0}^{q}=\Lambda_{0}^{q}\,T_{0}^{q}=\Lambda_{0}^{q}\,\delta_{2}^{-q}\,\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip where we used

in the last step.

Thus (
39
) follows with
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)=\Lambda_{0}\,\delta_{2}^{-1}
.

A comparison estimate up to boundary is stated below.
This provides an analogue of Lemma
3.1
.
The proof of this statement is similar to that of Lemma
3.1
and hence is omitted.

Lemma 4.3
.

be a weak solution of (

) and let

be as in (

). Then there are constants

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}(x_{1})}|Du-Dw|^{p}dx

2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2r}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}dx\right)^{2-p}.

Remark 4.4
.

As in Remark
3.3
we can choose
C^{\prime}=0
when
p\in[2,n)
.

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.5

We are now in a position to tackle Theorem
1.5
.
The following technical lemma, which can be found in
[
1
, Lemma 3.13]
(also cf.
[
21
, Lemma 3.4]
), will be in need.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\phi
be a nonnegative and nondecreasing functions on
(0,Z]
.
Let
A
,
B
,
\delta
,
\beta
be nonnegative constants with
\delta>\beta
and
\eta\in(0,1)
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\phi(\rho)\leq A\left[\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\delta}+\eta\right]\phi(R)+BR^{\beta}

for all
0<\rho\leq R\leq Z
.
Let
\gamma\in[\beta,\delta)
.
If
\eta<(2A)^{\delta/(\gamma-\delta)}
then there exists a
C=C(\delta,\beta,\gamma,A)>0
such that

\displaystyle\phi(\rho)\leq C\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\gamma}\phi(R)+CB\rho^{\beta}

for all
0<\rho\leq R\leq Z
.

Now we prove Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Let
r\leq r_{1}
and denote
B_{r}=B_{r}(x_{1})
.
Let
\sigma\in(0,n)
.
By Lemma
4.2
, there exists a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,p,\Lambda)\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dv|^{p}\,dz\leq C_{0}\epsilon^{\sigma}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|^{p}\,dz

for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/800)
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq 4^{p}\left(\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dv|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4^{p}\left(C_{0}\epsilon^{\sigma}\int_{B_{r}}|Dv|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}4^{p}\left(\epsilon^{\sigma}\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dv-Dw|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}4^{p}\left(\big[\epsilon^{\sigma}+\omega(r)^{\tau_{0}p}\big]\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dw|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz\right)

(46)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}4^{p}\left(\big[\epsilon^{\sigma}+\omega(r)^{\tau_{0}p}\big]\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz+\int_{B_{r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz\right)

for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/800)
, where we used the triangle inequality in the first, third and fifth steps as well as (
38
) in the fourth step.

Next we will use Lemma
4.3
to bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
46
).
Accordingly there exist constants
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,p)>0
and
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,p)\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Dw-Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left[\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}\,dz+\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np-n}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\left[\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}\,dz\right)^{p-1}+\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{n}}\right]\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz\right)^{2-p}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+\left(C_{1}+(p-1)C_{2}\left(\frac{\eta}{(C_{2}+1)(2-p)}\right)^{\frac{p-2}{p-1}}\right)\left[\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}\,dz+\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np-n}}\right]

for all
\eta>0
and
m\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where we used Young’s inequality in the last step.
As mentioned in Remark
4.4
we may take
C_{2}=0
when
p\in[2,n)
.

Consequently we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\epsilon r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{0}4^{p}\big[\epsilon^{\sigma}+\omega(r)^{\tau_{0}p}+\eta\big]\int_{B_{r}}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+2C_{0}4^{p}\left(C_{1}+(p-1)C_{2}\left(\frac{\eta}{(C_{2}+1)(2-p)}\right)^{\frac{p-2}{p-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left[\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}\,dz+\left(\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np-n}}\right]

for all
\epsilon\in(0,1/800)
,
\eta>0
and
m\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

By appropriately scaling the constants, we may choose
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
and
C_{\eta}=C_{\eta}(n,p,\Lambda,\eta)>0
such that the above estimate also holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,2)
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\sigma}+\omega(R)^{\tau_{0}p}+\eta\right]\,\int_{B_{R}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\eta}\,R^{\sigma}r_{1}^{n-\sigma}\int_{0}^{2{\rm diam(\Omega)}}\left[\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)}|f-m|^{p^{\prime}}\,dz+\rho^{p^{\prime}}\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{\rho}(x)}|g|^{\frac{np}{np-n+p}}\,dz\right)^{\frac{np-n+p}{np-n}}\right]\frac{d\rho}{\rho},

for all
0<\rho\leq R\leq 2r_{1}
,
\eta>0
and
m\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Now Lemma
4.5
with

\phi(r):=\int_{B_{r}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz\quad\mbox{with }r\in(0,2r_{1}),

gives

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\rho}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq C\,\left(\frac{\rho}{R}\right)^{\sigma}\int_{B_{R}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+C\,\rho^{\sigma}r_{1}^{n-\sigma}\int_{0}^{2{\rm diam(\Omega)}}\left[\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip provided that
r_{1}
and
\eta
are sufficiently small.
Hereafter
C=C(n,p,\Lambda)>0
.

Next let
x_{0}\in\Omega
.
A specific choice of
R=2r_{1}
and
\rho=2d(x_{0})
in the last display leads to

\displaystyle\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\displaystyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip-2.08334pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\textstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-2.08334pt\hskip-2.77774pt\raisebox{-1.65277pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.2pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.77774pt\hskip 2.08334pt}\kern-7.49994pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.72743pt\hskip-1.72743pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.72743pt\hskip-2.45831pt\raisebox{-1.15695pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.13998pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.45831pt\hskip 1.72743pt}\kern-6.78813pt}{\hbox{\hskip 1.57988pt\hskip-1.57988pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phantom{\int}$}$}\hskip-1.57988pt\hskip-2.29166pt\raisebox{-0.82639pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.09999pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}$}}\hskip-2.29166pt\hskip 1.57988pt}\kern-6.49301pt}\int_{B_{2d(x_{0})}(x_{1})}|Du|^{p}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{r_{1}}{d(x_{0})}\right)^{n-\sigma}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip \displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{d(x_{0})^{n-\sigma}}\int_{0}^{2{\rm diam(\Omega)}}\left[\,\mathchoice{\hbox{\hskip where we used (

) and the fact that

Now (

) follows directly from this last estimate and (

), which completes our proof.
